# QUESTION (read-only) — can any Alpha machine in this tree run a guest program at all?

## READ THIS FIRST — PLEASE DO NOT SIMPLY RATIFY

**Check the premises, not just the derivations.** Where I state something as fact, ask whether it is
true. A seat that re-derives a premise from something this brief did NOT give it is worth more than
one that agrees.

**THE TREE UNDER REVIEW IS `GXEMUL-SEC/`.** There are three pristine-2021-upstream copies of GXemul
under the project root (`./src/` and two under `_archive/audit-results-*/builds/`). They are NOT this
fork.

**SCOPE YOUR READING TO `GXEMUL-SEC/src/`, `GXEMUL-SEC/regress/*.py`, `GXEMUL-SEC/regress/*.sh` and
`GXEMUL-SEC/doc/`.** Do not read `GXEMUL-SEC/regress/artifacts/` or
`GXEMUL-SEC/tools/pipeline/ledger.json` — they are this project's archive of past measurements, they
are large, and nothing in them is needed here.

**VERDICT LINE FIRST**, so your answer survives truncation.

---

## Why I am asking

Four defects have been filed against the Alpha code paths in one night — a page-table walk that drops
the within-page offset, a PALcode register mismatch, an unsupported claim about fault delivery, and a
guest-reachable process exit. **All four are analysed and none has a witness.**

This project's rule is that a defect with no witness gets DOCUMENTED rather than fixed, and that a
round must attempt a real-guest reproduction before settling for that. So before any of these earns a
round, I need to know what is actually runnable.

**I would rather learn the answer is "nothing runs" now than after building a probe.**

## The questions

**Q1: which Alpha machine types does this tree define, and does any of them have a working boot
path?** Look at the machine descriptions and at what they require — a PROM image, a disk image, a
specific file that may or may not be present.

**Q2: does the regression harness exercise Alpha at all?** The gates live in `regress/*.sh` and the
probes in `regress/*.py`. If none mentions Alpha, that is itself the answer and I want it stated
plainly.

**Q3: can a guest program be run WITHOUT a full OS boot?** This project's witness ladder accepts a
"cold-debugger probe" — a real guest instruction executed through real address decode on an
unmodified in-tree machine description, driven by the built-in debugger rather than by booting an OS.
Several existing probes work that way. **Is that possible for an Alpha machine here** — can the
debugger plant instructions in memory, set a breakpoint, and continue, on some `-E` machine?

That third question is the one that matters most. A defect with no OS boot but a working
cold-debugger path is **witnessable**; a defect with neither is **documented-only**.

**Q4: for each of the four filed defects, which of the three is it?** Namely: reachable by a
cold-debugger probe / reachable only by a real OS boot / not reachable by anything runnable here.
I am not asking you to build the probe — only to say which category each falls in, and what the
blocker is where there is one.

## What I am not asking

Do not propose fixes. Do not write probes. **If the honest answer to Q3 is "no Alpha guest can
execute an instruction in this tree", say that plainly** — it is a genuinely useful answer and it
means four rows get documented and closed rather than sitting open pretending to be actionable.

**If, while verifying a claim, you found something ELSE — say so.**

regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:4: #
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:5: # This is the BREADTH gate. Everything else in this harness executes four machines; the
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:6: # fork changed 117 source files, 27 of them specific to architectures (alpha, ARM, i960,
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:7: # PowerPC, SGI) that no rig touches at all. This gate builds every machine type, attaches
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:8: # its devices, and lets ASan watch.
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:9: #
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:10: # It revives an instrument the project already used -- the CHANGELOG records a "run every
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:11: # machine under ASan sweep (23 machine types)" that found the macppc heap OOB (#23) -- but
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:12: # as a STANDING GATE rather than a one-off, and comparatively rather than absolutely.
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:13: #
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:14: # THE ASSERTION IS DIRECTIONAL, and that matters. The fork exists partly to FIX memory
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:15: # errors, so upstream being dirty where HEAD is clean is success, not failure. Only the
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:16: # reverse is a regression:
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:17: #
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:18: #     clean under pristine, dirty under HEAD   -> REGRESSION (this gate fails)
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:19: #     dirty under pristine, clean under HEAD   -> the fork doing its job
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:20: #     dirty under both                         -> pre-existing, reported not failed
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:21: #
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:22: # A MACHINE MUST BE HANDED A FILE OR IT NEVER CONSTRUCTS. Measured: `-E testmips` with no
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:23: # file prints usage and aborts before any device is attached -- a sweep built that way
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:24: # would test nothing and pass every time. With a dummy image the machine reports
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:25: # `model:`, `cpu:`, `memory:` and attaches devices. Machines with subtypes additionally
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:26: # need `-e <subtype>`; without it 15 of the 41 types never construct.  (This line said
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:27: # "13 of 37" until #444 re-measured it. The counts drift with the machine list; that is
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:28: # why nothing below asserts one.)
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:29: #
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:30: # ############################################################################
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:31: # #444 asansweepblind -- THREE MEASURED BLIND SPOTS, all of which left this gate GREEN
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:32: # while it was reporting things that were not true. Each fix below is annotated with the
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:33: # measurement that forced it; none of them is reasoned-from-source.
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:34: #
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:35: #  B1. IT COULD NOT SEE A CONSTRUCTION ABORT AT ALL.
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:36: #      sanhit() greps for ASan/UBSan text. A machine that calls abort() is not a
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:37: #      sanitizer hit, so it scored ZERO -- and the only construction assertion was a
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:38: #      FLOOR (`check_min "machine types constructed" 20`), which a handful of corpses
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:39: #      cannot breach.
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:40: #      MEASURED, by running the shipped gate against the same binaries this one uses:
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:41: #      **PASS (3 checks)** while 15 machine/subtype combinations were dying by SIGABRT.
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:42: #      It touched two of the fifteen (hpcmips/be-300, mvmeppc/mvme1600); both scored the
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:43: #      row `no  0  0` -- BYTE-IDENTICAL to the row a machine that merely wants a subtype
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:44: #      produces -- and both were absorbed by "machine types constructed 38 (>= 20)".
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:45: #      *** AND THE ABORT MESSAGE WAS NOT EVEN ON DISK. ***  core/interrupt.c:191-198 does
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:46: #      `printf("Aborting.\n"); abort();`, and abort() DOES NOT FLUSH STDIO. With stdout
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:47: #      redirected to a file glibc block-buffers it, so the whole diagnostic died with the
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:48: #      process: MEASURED, the log of `-E decstation -e 5400` was **0 bytes**. The gate then
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:49: #      read that empty file as `constructed=no, sanhit=0` -- INDISTINGUISHABLE from a
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:50: #      machine that merely needs a subtype. A dead machine and a fine one scored alike.
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:51: #      Fix: classify by EXIT STATUS (diedby(), below), which no buffering can lose, and
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:52: #      run under `stdbuf -o0 -e0` so the reason text survives for the human.
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:53: #
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:54: #  B2. IT SWEPT ONLY EACH TYPE'S *FIRST* SUBTYPE, so a defect in any later one was
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:55: #      invisible by construction. MEASURED: the shipped gate ran 14 subtypes in total,
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:56: #      one per type that needs one. Sweeping every subtype runs 58 and finds **15** dying
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:57: #      machines where a first-subtype-only sweep of the same 41 types finds **2** -- and

regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:59: #      harness had ever executed.

regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:62: #      bounded. If a future edit does bound it, the gate must SAY what it dropped -- do
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:63: #      not cap coverage silently.
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:64: #

regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:234: #    * LAST group, because a machine's *name* may itself contain quotes and parentheses --
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:235: #      `Generic "bare" ARM machine ("barearm")`, `ARM-based "Android" machines
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:236: #      ("android-arm")`, `DECstation 3100 (PMAX) ("pmax", "3100", "2100")`. Harvesting
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:237: #      every quoted token on the line would pick up `bare` and `Android`.
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:238: #    * SHELL-SAFE, because the alias becomes a word in an unquoted `for` list and a row
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:239: #      key. `silicon graphics` is the primary alias for SGI and is unusable as either;
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:240: #      `sgi` is the next one and is what the rest of this project calls it.
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:241: #  An unusable line emits X (type) or Y (subtype) and is asserted to zero below, so the
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:242: #  NEXT machine with a space in its primary alias is red rather than mis-attributed. A
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:243: #  failed type line also sets type="?" so its subtypes cannot silently inherit the
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:244: #  PREVIOUS type -- that inheritance is precisely how SGI's ten became rpi's ten.

regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:247:       p = 0

regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:249:       if (p == 0) return ""
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:250:       seg = substr(line, p)
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:251:       n = split(seg, arr, "\"")

regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:253:           if (arr[i] ~ /^[A-Za-z0-9_.+\/-]+$/) return arr[i]
regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:254:       return ""

src/devices/dev_lca.c:89: DEVICE_ACCESS(lca_pci_conf)
src/devices/dev_lca.c:90: {
src/devices/dev_lca.c:91: 	uint64_t idata = 0, odata = 0;
src/devices/dev_lca.c:92: 	int tag, bus, dev, func, reg;
src/devices/dev_lca.c:93: 	struct lca_data *d = (struct lca_data *) extra;
src/devices/dev_lca.c:94: 
src/devices/dev_lca.c:95: 	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
src/devices/dev_lca.c:96: 		idata = memory_readmax64(cpu, data, len);
src/devices/dev_lca.c:97: 
src/devices/dev_lca.c:98: 	/*
src/devices/dev_lca.c:99: 	 *  1. Decompose the address into a tag.
src/devices/dev_lca.c:100: 	 *
src/devices/dev_lca.c:101: 	 *  According to NetBSD's lca_pci.c, the address is composed like this:
src/devices/dev_lca.c:102: 	 *
src/devices/dev_lca.c:103: 	 *	addr = tag << 5 | (regoffset & ~0x03) << 5 | 0x3 << 3
src/devices/dev_lca.c:104: 	 */
src/devices/dev_lca.c:105: 	reg = (relative_addr >> 5) & 0xfc;
src/devices/dev_lca.c:106: 	tag = (relative_addr >> 5) & ~0xff;
src/devices/dev_lca.c:107: 
src/devices/dev_lca.c:108: 	/*
src/devices/dev_lca.c:109: 	 *  2. Decompose the tag into bus, dev, and func.
src/devices/dev_lca.c:110: 	 *
src/devices/dev_lca.c:111: 	 *  The tag can be constructed in one of two ways. On the primary
src/devices/dev_lca.c:112: 	 *  bus (nr 0):
src/devices/dev_lca.c:113: 	 *
src/devices/dev_lca.c:114: 	 *	tag = (1 << (device + 11)) | (function << 8);
src/devices/dev_lca.c:115: 	 *
src/devices/dev_lca.c:116: 	 *  and on other busses, the tag is a normal:
src/devices/dev_lca.c:117: 	 *
src/devices/dev_lca.c:118: 	 *	tag = (bus << 16) | (device << 11) | (function << 8)
src/devices/dev_lca.c:119: 	 */
src/devices/dev_lca.c:120: 	/*  printf("tag = 0x%x\n", (int)tag);  */
src/devices/dev_lca.c:121: 	bus = d->ioc_conf & 1;
src/devices/dev_lca.c:122: 
src/devices/dev_lca.c:123: 	if (bus == 0) {
src/devices/dev_lca.c:124: 		for (dev=0; dev<21; dev++)
src/devices/dev_lca.c:125: 			if (tag & (0x800 << dev))
src/devices/dev_lca.c:126: 				break;
src/devices/dev_lca.c:127: 		if (dev >= 21) {
src/devices/dev_lca.c:128: 			/*  fatal("[ LCA: No bus 0 device? TODO ]\n");
src/devices/dev_lca.c:129: 			exit(1);  */
src/devices/dev_lca.c:130: 			dev = 0;
src/devices/dev_lca.c:131: 		}
src/devices/dev_lca.c:132: 	} else {
src/devices/dev_lca.c:133: 		fatal("TODO. Non-zero bus.\n");
src/devices/dev_lca.c:134: 		exit(1);
src/devices/dev_lca.c:135: 	}
src/devices/dev_lca.c:136: 
src/devices/dev_lca.c:137: 	func = (tag >> 8) & 7;
src/devices/dev_lca.c:138: 	/*  printf("bus=%i dev=%i func=%i reg=%i\n", bus,dev,func,reg);  */
src/devices/dev_lca.c:139: 
src/devices/dev_lca.c:140: 	/*  Pass PCI accesses onto bus_pci:  */
src/devices/dev_lca.c:141: 	bus_pci_setaddr(cpu, d->pci_data, bus, dev, func, reg);
src/devices/dev_lca.c:142: 	bus_pci_data_access(cpu, d->pci_data, writeflag == MEM_READ?
src/devices/dev_lca.c:143: 	    &odata : &idata, len, writeflag);
src/devices/dev_lca.c:144: 
src/devices/dev_lca.c:145: 	if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
src/devices/dev_lca.c:146: 		memory_writemax64(cpu, data, len, odata);
src/devices/dev_lca.c:147: 
src/devices/dev_lca.c:148: 	return 1;
src/devices/dev_lca.c:149: }
src/devices/dev_lca.c:150: 
src/devices/dev_lca.c:151: 
src/devices/dev_lca.c:152: DEVICE_ACCESS(lca_isa)
src/devices/dev_lca.c:153: {
src/devices/dev_lca.c:154: 	unsigned int ofs, i;
src/devices/dev_lca.c:155: 	uint8_t byte;
src/devices/dev_lca.c:156: 
src/devices/dev_lca.c:157: 	relative_addr >>= 5;
src/devices/dev_lca.c:158: 
src/devices/dev_lca.c:159: 	ofs = relative_addr & 3;
src/devices/dev_lca.c:160: 	if (ofs > len) {
src/devices/dev_lca.c:161: 		fatal("[ ofs=%i len=%i in lca_isa access function. "
src/devices/dev_lca.c:162: 		    "aborting ]\n", ofs, len);
src/devices/dev_lca.c:163: 		exit(1);
src/devices/dev_lca.c:164: 	}
src/devices/dev_lca.c:165: 
src/devices/dev_lca.c:166: 	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
src/devices/dev_lca.c:167: 		byte = data[ofs % len];
src/devices/dev_lca.c:168: 		return cpu->memory_rw(cpu, cpu->mem, LCA_ISA_BASE +
src/devices/dev_lca.c:169: 		    relative_addr, &byte, 1, writeflag, CACHE_NONE);
src/devices/dev_lca.c:170: 	}
src/devices/dev_lca.c:171: 
src/devices/dev_lca.c:172: 	cpu->memory_rw(cpu, cpu->mem, LCA_ISA_BASE + relative_addr,
src/devices/dev_lca.c:173: 	    &byte, 1, MEM_READ, CACHE_NONE);
src/devices/dev_lca.c:174: 
src/devices/dev_lca.c:175: 	for (i=0; i<len; i++)
src/devices/dev_lca.c:176: 		data[i] = i == ofs? byte : 0x00;
src/devices/dev_lca.c:177: 
src/devices/dev_lca.c:178: 	return 1;
src/devices/dev_lca.c:179: }

935: void DYNTRANS_INIT_TABLES(struct cpu *cpu)
936: {
937: #ifndef MODE32
938: 	struct DYNTRANS_L2_64_TABLE *dummy_l2;
939: 	struct DYNTRANS_L3_64_TABLE *dummy_l3;

946: 	    (struct DYNTRANS_TC_PHYSPAGE *) malloc(sizeof(struct DYNTRANS_TC_PHYSPAGE)));
947:

949: 	ppp->translations_bitmap = 0;
950: 	ppp->translation_ranges_ofs = 0;
951: 	/*  ppp->physaddr is filled in by the page allocator  */
952: 
953: 	for (i=0; i<DYNTRANS_IC_ENTRIES_PER_PAGE; i++)
954: 		ppp->ics[i].f = TO_BE_TRANSLATED;
955: 
956: 	/*  End-of-page:  */
957: 	ppp->ics[DYNTRANS_IC_ENTRIES_PER_PAGE + 0].f =
958: #ifdef DYNTRANS_DUALMODE_32
959: 	    cpu->is_32bit? instr32(end_of_page) :
960: #endif
961: 	    instr(end_of_page);
962: 
963: 	/*  End-of-page-2, for delay-slot architectures:  */
964: #ifdef DYNTRANS_DELAYSLOT
965: 	ppp->ics[DYNTRANS_IC_ENTRIES_PER_PAGE + 1].f =
966: #ifdef DYNTRANS_DUALMODE_32
967: 	    cpu->is_32bit? instr32(end_of_page2) :
968: #endif
969: 	    instr(end_of_page2);
970: #endif
971: 
972: 	cpu->cd.DYNTRANS_ARCH.physpage_template = ppp;
973: 
974: 
975: 	/*  Prepare 64-bit virtual address translation tables:  */
976: #ifndef MODE32
977: 	if (cpu->is_32bit)
978: 		return;
979: 
980: 	dummy_l2 = (struct DYNTRANS_L2_64_TABLE *) zeroed_alloc(sizeof(struct DYNTRANS_L2_64_TABLE));
981: 	dummy_l3 = (struct DYNTRANS_L3_64_TABLE *) zeroed_alloc(sizeof(struct DYNTRANS_L3_64_TABLE));
982: 
983: 	cpu->cd.DYNTRANS_ARCH.l2_64_dummy = dummy_l2;
984: 	cpu->cd.DYNTRANS_ARCH.l3_64_dummy = dummy_l3;
985: 
986: 	for (x1 = 0; x1 < (1 << DYNTRANS_L1N); x1 ++)
987: 		cpu->cd.DYNTRANS_ARCH.l1_64[x1] = dummy_l2;
988: 
989: 	for (x2 = 0; x2 < (1 << DYNTRANS_L2N); x2 ++)
990: 		dummy_l2->l3[x2] = dummy_l3;
991: #endif
992: }
993: #endif	/*  DYNTRANS_INIT_TABLES  */

41:  *  memory_rw():
42:  *
43:  *  Read or write data from/to memory.
44:  *
45:  *	cpu		the cpu doing the read/write

50:  *	len		the length of the 'data' buffer
51:  *	writeflag	set to MEM_READ or MEM_WRITE
52:  *	misc_flags	CACHE_{NONE,DATA,INSTRUCTION} | other flags
53:  *
54:  *  If the address indicates access to a memory mapped device, that device'
55:  *  read/write access function is called.
56:  *
57:  *  This function should not be called with cpu == NULL.
58:  *
59:  *  Returns one of the following:
60:  *	MEMORY_ACCESS_FAILED
61:  *	MEMORY_ACCESS_OK
62:  *
63:  *  (MEMORY_ACCESS_FAILED is 0.)
64:  */
65: 
66: /*
67:  *  #430: THE PAGE SIZE, IN ONE PLACE.
68:  *
69:  *  It was a local inside the function.  The wrapper below needs the same number, and two
70:  *  copies of a page size that can silently diverge is the shape this tree has been bitten
71:  *  by before -- so it is hoisted to a macro and the local now reads it.  Note the Alpha
72:  *  value: it is 8 KB, not 4 KB, so anything that splits at "the page boundary" must use
73:  *  THIS and never a literal 4096.  A review seat caught exactly that hazard in the design
74:  *  brief, which had said "4 KB page" throughout.
75:  */
76: #ifdef MEM_ALPHA
77: #define	MRW_OFFSET_MASK		0x1fff
78: #else
79: #define	MRW_OFFSET_MASK		0xfff
80: #endif
81: 
82: /*
83:  *  #430: THE SPLIT GRANULE IS 1 KB, AND IT IS NOT THE SAME NUMBER AS THE MASK ABOVE.
84:  *
85:  *  MRW_OFFSET_MASK is the HOST-page granule: what memory_paddr_to_hostaddr() is asked for
86:  *  and what update_translation_table() caches.  The granule the SPLIT needs is a different
87:  *  thing -- the smallest unit at which a translator in this tree can send two adjacent
88:  *  virtual addresses to unrelated physical ones -- and it is SMALLER:
89:  *
90:  *      memory_arm.c:198-207   descriptor type 3 on a non-XScale core is a 1 KB page,
91:  *                             *return_paddr = (d2 & 0xfffffc00) | (vaddr & 0x3ff)
92:  *      memory_sh.c:116        SH4_PTEL_SZ_1K gives mask 0xfffffc00
93:  *      memory_mips_v2p.c      the VR41xx path likewise has 1 KB logic
94:  *
95:  *  A first draft of this split used the 4 KB mask and a review seat produced the
96:  *  counterexample: with a 1 KB mapping, a four-byte access at 0x13fe crosses a translation
97:  *  boundary but NOT a 4 KB one, so the loop never fired and the defect survived exactly
98:  *  where MEMORY_NOT_FULL_PAGE says it is most likely.  Splitting finer is always SAFE --
99:  *  each chunk is then inside one mapping for every translator here, and the worker's own
100:  *  host-page arithmetic is unchanged -- so the only cost is more worker calls on the slow
101:  *  path for a multi-page translated access, which is rare (large transfers are PHYSICAL
102:  *  and exempt).  Correctness at a known granule beats a faster one that is wrong on ARM.
103:  */
104: #define	MRW_SPLIT_MASK		0x3ff
105: 
106: /*  The one-page worker; its name is derived from MEMORY_RW because cpu_alpha.c includes
107:     this file TWICE in one translation unit (alpha_userland_memory_rw, then alpha_memory_rw
108:     via tmp_alpha_tail.c), so a plainly named static would be a redefinition.  */
109: #ifndef MRW_CAT2
110: #define	MRW_CAT2(a,b)		a##b
111: #define	MRW_CAT(a,b)		MRW_CAT2(a,b)
112: #endif
113: #define	MEMORY_RW_ONE_PAGE	MRW_CAT(MEMORY_RW, _one_page)
114: 
115: /*
116:  *  The one relationship between the two that MUST hold, asserted at COMPILE TIME because it
117:  *  cannot be asserted at run time by the offline differential.
118:  *
119:  *  MEM_ALPHA is never compiled by that differential -- its fixture is 4 KB-specific -- so a
120:  *  review seat MEASURED that widening MRW_OFFSET_MASK, or hardcoding the worker's local back
121:  *  to 0xfff (exactly the drift this macro exists to prevent), leaves every row green.  A
122:  *  static assertion costs nothing and covers the Alpha build the test cannot reach.  The
123:  *  name is derived because this file is included twice per translation unit.
124:  */
125: typedef char MRW_CAT(MEMORY_RW, _split_le_page)
126:     [(MRW_SPLIT_MASK) <= (MRW_OFFSET_MASK) ? 1 : -1];
127: 
128: static int MEMORY_RW_ONE_PAGE(struct cpu *cpu, struct memory *mem, uint64_t vaddr,
129: 	unsigned char *data, size_t len, int writeflag, int misc_flags);
130: 
131: 
132: /*
133:  *  #430: TRANSLATE ONCE PER PAGE, NOT ONCE PER ACCESS.
134:  *
135:  *  The worker below translates the START vaddr and then copies the whole length from the
136:  *  host pointer for THAT page.  memory_paddr_to_hostaddr() returns a pointer into a 1 MB
137:  *  memblock, so the copy walks host memory that is contiguous PHYSICALLY while the guest's
138:  *  span is contiguous VIRTUALLY.  Those coincide only by luck, and the moment the length
139:  *  carries past the page boundary the guest reads -- or OVERWRITES -- whatever physical
140:  *  page happens to follow.  Measured offline against a deliberately non-monotonic map: a
141:  *  straddling read returned the physically-following page's bytes, and a straddling WRITE
142:  *  left the guest's intended page untouched while corrupting an unrelated one.  Measured
143:  *  too: when the tail page had no valid translation the old code returned
144:  *  MEMORY_ACCESS_OK anyway, so this was a MISSING FAULT as well as a wrong page.
145:  *
146:  *  The existing split at the end of the worker is NOT this guard and must not be mistaken
147:  *  for it: it fires at BITS_PER_MEMBLOCK (1 MB) granularity and its job is to stop the memcpy
148:  *  running off the end of a host allocation.
149:  *
150:  *  It is ONE BOUNDARY IN 1024, not 256: MRW_SPLIT_MASK is 0x3ff, so the split granule is
151:  *  1024 bytes and 1 MB / 1024 = 1024.  The old "256" came from dividing 1 MB by a 4 KB page
152:  *  -- a granule this split does not use, as the note above says: "The granule the SPLIT
153:  *  needs is a different one."  (And per that note the real page here is 8 KB anyway.)
154:  *
155:  *  Two properties of the memblock split, both MEASURED:
156:  *    - post-#430 it fires only on PHYSICAL accesses.  A TRANSLATED 4096-byte write across a
157:  *      1 MB boundary is chunked at the 1024-byte SPLIT granule, so it costs 4 translations
158:  *      and 4 host lookups and never reaches here; PHYSICAL costs 0 and 2.  A chunk of at
159:  *      most one granule, granule-aligned, cannot cross 1 MB.
160:  *    - its head can NEVER be a device, in either version: device dispatch `goto
161:  *      do_return_ok`s first.  Measured with a device straddling the memblock boundary --
162:  *      one device call, offset 0xffe, len 4, zero host lookups.
163:  *
164:  *  A LOOP, not recursion.  A recursive split costs a real frame per chunk when the compiler
165:  *  does NOT eliminate the tail call -- measured at -O0, 1,834,896 bytes of stack for a 16 MB
166:  *  access -- and a large enough access then dies on the stack rather than returning an
167:  *  answer.
168:  *
169:  *  CORRECTION, and it is the reason this paragraph is worded carefully: the first draft said
170:  *  "gcc does not turn the tail call into a loop at -O2".  MEASURED, IT DOES -- gcc 15.2.1
171:  *  performs the sibling-call optimisation at -O2 and -O3, leaving a 96-byte span.  So the
172:  *  loop is still the right implementation (it does not depend on an optimisation being
173:  *  applied, and -O0 builds and other compilers are real), but the justification was wrong,
174:  *  and the row that certified it was VACUOUS until the differential was given
175:  *  -fno-optimize-sibling-calls.  No caller in the tree passes an access large enough for
176:  *  this to bite today; it is insurance, and it costs about fifteen lines.
177:  *
178:  *  ONLY WHEN A TRANSLATION ACTUALLY HAPPENS.  Under PHYSICAL (or with no translate_v2p)
179:  *  paddr == vaddr, there is no per-page mapping to redo, and splitting would buy nothing
180:  *  while turning one large DMA into thousands of calls -- so those go straight through and
181:  *  the memblock split keeps doing its job unchanged.
182:  */
306: 	{

308: 		static int32_t start = -1, stop = -1;

717: 	/*
718: 	 *  Uncached access:
719: 	 *
720: 	 *  1)  Translate the physical address to a host address.
721: 	 *
722: 	 *  2)  Insert this virtual->physical->host translation into the
723: 	 *      fast translation arrays (using update_translation_table()).
724: 	 *
725: 	 *  3)  If this was a Write, then invalidate any code translations
726: 	 *      in that page.
727: 	 */
728: 	memblock = memory_paddr_to_hostaddr(mem, paddr & ~offset_mask,
729: 	    writeflag);
730: 	if (memblock == NULL) {
731: 		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
732: 			memset(data, 0, len);

735: 
736: 	offset = paddr & offset_mask;
737:

742:             !(cpu->cd.mips.coproc[0]->reg[COP0_STATUS] & MIPS1_ISOL_CACHES))
743: #endif
744: 	    && !(ok & MEMORY_NOT_FULL_PAGE)
745: 	    && !no_exceptions)

747: 		    memblock, (misc_flags & MEMORY_USER_ACCESS) |

763: 		/*  #165: (Codex/Fable) split accesses that cross a memblock
764: 		    boundary (e.g. large DMA transfers) at the boundary,

767: 		    (paddr & ((1<<BITS_PER_MEMBLOCK)-1)));

775: 		    len - len_head, writeflag, misc_flags);

779: 	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)

781: 	else

767: /*
768:  *  alpha_instr_to_be_translated():
769:  *
770:  *  Translate an instruction word into an alpha_instr_call. ic is filled in with
771:  *  valid data for the translated instruction, or a "nothing" instruction if
772:  *  there was a translation failure. The newly translated instruction is then
773:  *  executed.
774:  */
775: X(to_be_translated)
776: {
777: 	uint64_t addr, low_pc;
778: 	uint32_t iword;
779: 	unsigned char *page;
780: 	unsigned char ib[4];
781: 	void (*samepage_function)(struct cpu *, struct alpha_instr_call *);
782: 	int opcode, ra, rb, func, rc, imm, load, loadstore_type, fp, llsc;
783: 
784: 	/*  Figure out the (virtual) address of the instruction:  */
785: 	low_pc = ((size_t)ic - (size_t)cpu->cd.alpha.cur_ic_page)
786: 	    / sizeof(struct alpha_instr_call);
787: 	addr = cpu->pc & ~((ALPHA_IC_ENTRIES_PER_PAGE-1) <<
788: 	    ALPHA_INSTR_ALIGNMENT_SHIFT);
789: 	addr += (low_pc << ALPHA_INSTR_ALIGNMENT_SHIFT);
790: 	addr &= ~((1 << ALPHA_INSTR_ALIGNMENT_SHIFT) - 1);
791: 	cpu->pc = addr;
792: 
793: 	/*  Read the instruction word from memory:  */
794: 	{
795: 		const uint32_t mask1 = (1 << DYNTRANS_L1N) - 1;
796: 		const uint32_t mask2 = (1 << DYNTRANS_L2N) - 1;
797: 		const uint32_t mask3 = (1 << DYNTRANS_L3N) - 1;
798: 		uint32_t x1 = (addr >> (64-DYNTRANS_L1N)) & mask1;
799: 		uint32_t x2 = (addr >> (64-DYNTRANS_L1N-DYNTRANS_L2N)) & mask2;
800: 		uint32_t x3 = (addr >> (64-DYNTRANS_L1N-DYNTRANS_L2N-
801: 		    DYNTRANS_L3N)) & mask3;
802: 		struct DYNTRANS_L2_64_TABLE *l2 = cpu->cd.alpha.l1_64[x1];
803: 		struct DYNTRANS_L3_64_TABLE *l3 = l2->l3[x2];
804: 		page = l3->host_load[x3];
805: 	}
806: 
807: 	if (page != NULL) {
808: 		/*  fatal("TRANSLATION HIT!\n");  */
809: 		memcpy(ib, page + (addr & 8191), sizeof(ib));
810: 	} else {
811: 		/*  fatal("TRANSLATION MISS!\n");  */
812: 		if (!cpu->memory_rw(cpu, cpu->mem, addr, &ib[0],
813: 		    sizeof(ib), MEM_READ, CACHE_INSTRUCTION)) {
814: 			fatal("to_be_translated(): read failed: TODO\n");
815: 			goto bad;
816: 		}
817: 	}
818: 
819: 	/*  Alpha instruction words are always little-endian. Convert
820: 	    to host order:  */

822: 		uint32_t *p = (uint32_t *) ib;
823: 		iword = LE32_TO_HOST( *p );
824: 	}
825: 
826: #define DYNTRANS_TO_BE_TRANSLATED_HEAD
827: #include "cpu_dyntrans.c"
828: #undef	DYNTRANS_TO_BE_TRANSLATED_HEAD
829: 
830: 
831: 	opcode = (iword >> 26) & 63;
832: 	ra = (iword >> 21) & 31;

836: 	imm = iword & 0xffff;

838: 	switch (opcode) {
839: 	case 0x00:						/*  CALL_PAL  */

844: 	case 0x09:						/*  LDAH  */

846: 			ic->f = instr(nop);
847: 			break;
848: 		}
849: 		/*  TODO: A special case which is common is to add or subtract

28:  *  COMMENT: Raw file support
29:  *
30:  *  (Simply loads raw data into emulated memory space).
31:  */
32: 
33: /*  Note: Included from file.c.  */
34: 
35: 
36: /*
37:  *  file_load_raw():
38:  *
39:  *  Loads a raw binary into emulated memory. The filename should be
40:  *  of the following form:     loadaddress:filename
41:  *  or    loadaddress:skiplen:filename
42:  *  or    loadaddress:skiplen:pc:filename
43:  */
44: static void file_load_raw(struct machine *m, struct memory *mem,
45: 	char *filename, uint64_t *entrypointp)
46: {
47: 	FILE *f;
48: 	int len, sign3264;
49: 	unsigned char buf[16384];
50: 	uint64_t entry, loadaddr, vaddr, skip = 0;
51: 	char *p, *p2;
52: 
53: 	/*  Special case for 32-bit MIPS:  */
54: 	sign3264 = 0;
55: 	if (m->cpus[0]->cpu_family->arch == ARCH_MIPS && m->cpus[0]->is_32bit)
56: 		sign3264 = 1;
57: 
58: 	p = strchr(filename, ':');
59: 	if (p == NULL) {
60: 		fprintf(stderr, "\n");
61: 		perror(filename);
62: 		exit(1);
63: 	}
64: 
65: 	loadaddr = vaddr = entry = strtoull(filename, NULL, 0);
66: 	p2 = p+1;
67: 
68: 	/*  A second value? That's the optional skip value  */
69: 	p = strchr(p2, ':');
70: 	if (p != NULL) {
71: 		skip = strtoull(p2, NULL, 0);
72: 		p = p+1;
73: 		/*  A third value? That's the initial pc:  */
74: 		if (strchr(p, ':') != NULL) {
75: 			entry = strtoull(p, NULL, 0);
76: 			p = strchr(p, ':') + 1;
77: 		}
78: 	} else
79: 		p = p2;
80: 
81: 	if (sign3264) {
82: 		loadaddr = (int64_t)(int32_t)loadaddr;
83: 		entry = (int64_t)(int32_t)entry;
84: 		vaddr = (int64_t)(int32_t)vaddr;
85: 		skip = (int64_t)(int32_t)skip;
86: 	}
87: 
88: 	f = fopen(strrchr(filename, ':')+1, "r");
89: 	if (f == NULL) {
90: 		perror(p);
91: 		exit(1);
92: 	}
93: 
94: 	fseek(f, skip, SEEK_SET);
95: 
96: 	/*  Load file contents:  */
97: 	while (!feof(f)) {
98: 		size_t to_read = sizeof(buf);
99: 
100: 		/*  If vaddr isn't buf-size aligned, then start with a
101: 		    smaller buffer:  */
102: 		if (vaddr & (sizeof(buf) - 1))
103: 			to_read = sizeof(buf) - (vaddr & (sizeof(buf)-1));
104: 
105: 		len = fread(buf, 1, to_read, f);
106: 
107: 		if (len > 0)
108: 			m->cpus[0]->memory_rw(m->cpus[0], mem, vaddr, &buf[0],
109: 			    len, MEM_WRITE, NO_EXCEPTIONS);
110: 
111: 		vaddr += len;
112: 	}
113: 
114: 	debug("RAW: 0x%" PRIx64" bytes @ 0x%08" PRIx64,
115: 	    (uint64_t) (ftello(f) - skip), (uint64_t) loadaddr);
116: 
117: 	if (skip != 0)
118: 		debug(" (0x%" PRIx64" bytes of header skipped)",
119: 		    (uint64_t) skip);
120: 
121: 	debug("\n");
122: 
123: 	fclose(f);
124: 
125: 	*entrypointp = entry;
126: 
127: 	n_executables_loaded ++;
128: }

42:  *  debugger_cmd_breakpoint():
43:  *
44:  *  The subcommand name may be abbreviated to any unambiguous prefix,
45:  *  e.g. "breakpoint s" == "breakpoint show".
46:  */
47: static void debugger_cmd_breakpoint(struct machine *m, char *args)
48: {
49: 	static const char *subcmd_name[] = { "add", "delete", "show",
50: 	    "subsystem" };
51: 	const int SUBCMD_ADD = 0, SUBCMD_DELETE = 1, SUBCMD_SHOW = 2,
52: 	    SUBCMD_SUBSYSTEM = 3;
53: 	const int n_subcmds = 4;
54: 	int i, res, subcmd = -1, n_matches = 0;
55: 	size_t wordlen;
56: 	char *rest;
57: 
58: 	while (args[0] != '\0' && args[0] == ' ')
59: 		args ++;
60: 
61: 	if (args[0] == '\0') {
62: 		printf("syntax: breakpoint subcmd [args...]\n");
63: 		printf("Available subcmds (and args) are:\n");
64: 		printf("  add addr[, N] add a breakpoint for address addr"
65: 		    " (N = ignore first N hits)\n");
66: 		printf("  delete x      delete breakpoint nr x\n");
67: 		printf("  show          show current breakpoints\n");
68: 		printf("  subsystem [name error|warning|info|debug|off]\n");
69: 		printf("                show or set breakpoints on subsystem"
70: 		    " messages\n");
71: 		printf("Subcmds may be abbreviated to any unambiguous prefix,"
72: 		    " e.g. \"breakpoint sh\".\n");
73: 		return;
74: 	}
75: 
76: 	/*  The first word of args is the subcommand. It may be abbreviated
77: 	    to any prefix which matches exactly one of the subcommands:  */
78: 	wordlen = 0;
79: 	while (args[wordlen] != '\0' && args[wordlen] != ' ')
80: 		wordlen ++;
81: 
82: 	for (i=0; i<n_subcmds; i++)
83: 		if (strncasecmp(args, subcmd_name[i], wordlen) == 0) {
94: 		    (int) wordlen, args);
95: 		return;
96: 	}
97:

184: 	unsigned char *data, size_t len, int writeflag, int misc_flags)
185: {
186: 	if (!(misc_flags & PHYSICAL) && cpu->translate_v2p != NULL) {
187: 		size_t granule = (size_t) MRW_SPLIT_MASK + 1;
188: 		size_t offset = (size_t) (vaddr & MRW_SPLIT_MASK);
189: 
190: 		/*  `len > granule - offset`, NOT `offset + len > granule`: the sum can
191: 		    wrap for an absurd length (offset 1, len SIZE_MAX), which would skip
192: 		    the loop and hand the whole thing to the worker.  The subtraction
193: 		    cannot wrap, because offset < granule always.
194: 
195: 		    STATED EXACTLY, because the first version of this comment implied more
196: 		    than it delivers: this form does not SERVICE such a length, it declines
197: 		    to mis-copy it.  A length near SIZE_MAX now iterates about 2^54 times
198: 		    instead of silently under-splitting -- measured, a fuzz watchdog fired
199: 		    at two million chunks.  No caller passes anything remotely like it (the
200: 		    largest in the tree are PHYSICAL and exempt), so the case is recorded
201: 		    and filed rather than handled.  */
202: 		while (len > granule - offset) {
203: 			size_t head = granule - offset;
204: 
205: 			if (!MEMORY_RW_ONE_PAGE(cpu, mem, vaddr, data, head,
206: 			    writeflag, misc_flags)) {
207: 				/*  #244 PROMISES THE WHOLE READ BUFFER, AND SPLITTING BROKE
208: 				    THAT: the worker zeroes only the chunk IT was given, so a
209: 				    failure before the last chunk used to leave the caller's
210: 				    remaining bytes untouched -- uninitialised host stack, for
211: 				    a caller that ignores the return value.  Unsplit, one call
212: 				    covered the whole length.  Zero the rest here so the
213: 				    guarantee is the same as it was.  */
214: 				if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
215: 					memset(data, 0, len);
216: 				return MEMORY_ACCESS_FAILED;
217: 			}
218: 
219: 			vaddr += head;
220: 			data += head;
221: 			len -= head;
222: 			offset = 0;
223: 		}
224: 	}
225: 
226: 	return MEMORY_RW_ONE_PAGE(cpu, mem, vaddr, data, len, writeflag, misc_flags);
227: }

230: static int MEMORY_RW_ONE_PAGE(struct cpu *cpu, struct memory *mem, uint64_t vaddr,
231: 	unsigned char *data, size_t len, int writeflag, int misc_flags)
232: {

252: 		    + (cache==CACHE_INSTRUCTION? FLAG_INSTR : 0));

258: 		 */
259: 		if (!ok) {

263: 			    PROM string helpers) consume deterministic 0 bytes
264: 			    instead of uninitialised host stack. Same zero-fill
265: 			    ethos as the failed-device-read path (#95).  */
266: 			if (writeflag == MEM_READ)

276: 	 *  reaches this slow path is covered. Watched pages are kept off the
277: 	 *  fast store table by update_translation_table(), and R3000 cached
278: 	 *  data already comes through here. Show the store (writer pc, target,
279: 	 *  width, value) and request entry into the debugger after the current
280: 	 *  instruction, via the same about_to_enter_single_step mechanism as

282: 	 */

63:  *  For some emulation modes, it is possible to boot from a harddisk image by
64:  *  loading a bootblock from a specific disk offset into memory, and executing
65:  *  that, instead of requiring a separate kernel file.  It is then up to the
66:  *  bootblock to load a kernel.
67:  *
68:  *  Returns 1 on success, 0 on failure.
69:  */

regress/arm_flags_probe.py
1: #!/usr/bin/env python3
2: """Gate 14's measuring instrument: ARM subtract-carry flags and undefined routing.
3: 
4: Prints one line per row:  <name> <got> <want> ok|FAIL
5: then ARM_FLAGS_CONTROL=OK|DEAD and ARM_FLAGS_RESULT=<passed>/<total>.
6: gate_arm.sh does the asserting.
7: 
8: How it measures
9: ---------------
10: Cold debugger on `testarm` (SA1110), RAM at 0. The machine needs A file to
11: construct -- it prints usage and exits without one -- so a four-byte raw stub is
12: loaded at the code address purely to satisfy that; it is overwritten by every
13: row before anything executes. LITTLE-endian, so `dump` renders bytes in memory
14: order and every word read here is byte-swapped back (the PowerPC rig is the
15: opposite case, and mixing the two up silently transposes every answer).
16: 
17: The flags are read by the guest's OWN `mrs r3,cpsr`, never by a debugger
18: register print: `mrs` is the architectural read, and it is the same path a real
19: kernel uses. The result register is stored alongside, because these two defects
103: ADCS = 0xE0B10002               # adcs r0,r1,r2
104: 
105: UDF        = 0xE7F000F0         # permanently undefined (UDF #0)
106: UDF_GDB    = 0xE6000010         # the GDB-breakpoint form, same space
107: UDF_EQ     = 0x07F000F0         # the same encoding, condition EQ
108: ARM_MODE_UND32 = 0x1b
109: 
110: 
111: def session(cmds, nwords):
112:     """Run one cold-debugger session; return (words, transcript)."""
113:     #  -A disables colorized output. Under pty.fork() both isatty() checks
114:     #  pass, so a CLICOLOR in the caller's environment would otherwise put
115:     #  ANSI escapes between "cpu " and the debugmsg name and silently fail
116:     #  every marker grep -- an environment-dependent FAIL that looks like a
117:     #  real regression (a round-99 review seat's catch).
118:     pid, fd = pty.fork()
119:     if pid == 0:
120:         os.execvp(BIN, [BIN, "-V", "-A", "-E", "testarm", "-M", "64",
121:                         "0x%x:%s" % (CODE, STUB)])
122:         os._exit(127)
123:     buf = ""
124: 
125:     def rd(t=0.4):
126:         nonlocal buf
127:         r, _, _ = select.select([fd], [], [], t)
128:         if fd not in r:
129:             return True
130:         try:
131:             d = os.read(fd, 65536)
132:         except OSError:
133:             return False
134:         if not d:
135:             return False
136:         buf += d.decode("latin1", "replace")
137:         return True
138: 
139:     def wait(timeout=45):

src/core/interrupt.c
166: int interrupt_handler_lookup(const char *name, struct interrupt *templ)
167: {
168: 	int i;
169: 
170: #ifdef INTERRUPT_DEBUG
171: 	printf("interrupt_handler_lookup(\"%s\")\n", name);
172: #endif
173: 
174: 	if (name[0] == '\0') {
175: 		/*  No interrupt:  */
176: 		memset(templ, 0, sizeof(struct interrupt));
177: 		templ->interrupt_assert = no_interrupt_assert;
178: 		templ->interrupt_deassert = no_interrupt_deassert;
179: 	}
180: 
181: 	for (i=0; i<nr_of_interrupt_handlers; i++) {
182: 		if (strcmp(name, interrupt_handlers[i].templ.name) != 0)
183: 			continue;
184: 
185: 		*templ = interrupt_handlers[i].templ;
186: 		return 1;
187: 	}
188: 
189: 	/*  The 'if' is an ugly hack to prevent a Compaq CC warning.  */
190: 	if (i >= nr_of_interrupt_handlers) {
191: 		printf("interrupt_handler_lookup(\"%s\") failed.\n", name);
192: 
193: 		printf("Available handler paths are:\n");
194: 		for (i=0; i<nr_of_interrupt_handlers; i++)
195: 			printf("    %s\n", interrupt_handlers[i].templ.name);
196: 
197: 		printf("Aborting.\n");
198: 		abort();
199: 	}
200: 
201: 	return 0;

113:  *  zeroed_alloc():
114:  *
115:  *  Allocates a block of memory using mmap(), and if that fails, try
116:  *  malloc() + memset(). The returned memory block contains only zeroes.
117:  */
118: void *zeroed_alloc(size_t s)
119: {
120: 	void *p = mmap(NULL, s, PROT_READ | PROT_WRITE,
121: 	    MAP_ANON | MAP_PRIVATE, -1, 0);
122: 
123: 	/*  #175: (Codex/Fable) mmap failure is MAP_FAILED, not NULL  */
124: 	if (p == MAP_FAILED) {
125: #if 1

127: 		    " not usually happen. If you can reproduce this, then"
128: 		    " please contact me with details about your run-time"
129: 		    " environment.\n");
130: 		exit(1);
131: #else
132: 		CHECK_ALLOCATION(p = malloc(s));
133: 		memset(p, 0, s);
134: #endif
135: 	}
136: 
137: 	return p;
138: }

142:  *  memory_new():
143:  *
144:  *  This function creates a new memory object. An emulated machine needs one
145:  *  of these.
146:  */
147: struct memory *memory_new(uint64_t physical_max)
148: {
149: 	struct memory *mem;
150: 	int bits_per_pagetable = BITS_PER_PAGETABLE;
151: 	int bits_per_memblock = BITS_PER_MEMBLOCK;
152: 	int entries_per_pagetable = 1 << BITS_PER_PAGETABLE;
153: 	int max_bits = MAX_BITS;
154: 	size_t s;
155: 
156: 	CHECK_ALLOCATION(mem = (struct memory *) malloc(sizeof(struct memory)));
157: 	memset(mem, 0, sizeof(struct memory));
158: 
159: 	/*  Check bits_per_pagetable and bits_per_memblock for sanity:  */
160: 	if (bits_per_pagetable + bits_per_memblock != max_bits) {
161: 		fprintf(stderr, "memory_new(): bits_per_pagetable and "
162: 		    "bits_per_memblock mismatch\n");
163: 		exit(1);
164: 	}
165: 
166: 	mem->physical_max = physical_max;
169: 	s = entries_per_pagetable * sizeof(void *);
170: 
171: 	mem->pagetable = (unsigned char *) mmap(NULL, s,
172: 	    PROT_READ | PROT_WRITE, MAP_ANON | MAP_PRIVATE, -1, 0);
173: 	/*  #175: (Codex/Fable) mmap failure is MAP_FAILED, not NULL  */
174: 	if (mem->pagetable == MAP_FAILED) {
175: 		CHECK_ALLOCATION(mem->pagetable = malloc(s));
176: 		memset(mem->pagetable, 0, s);
177: 	}
178: 
179: 	mem->mmap_dev_minaddr = 0xffffffffffffffffULL;
180: 	mem->mmap_dev_maxaddr = 0;
181: 
182: 	return mem;
183: }

191:  */
192: int memory_points_to_string(struct cpu *cpu, struct memory *mem, uint64_t addr,
193: 	int min_string_length)
194: {
195: 	int cur_length = 0;
196: 	unsigned char c;
197: 
198: 	for (;;) {

200: 		cpu->memory_rw(cpu, mem, addr+cur_length,
201: 		    &c, sizeof(c), MEM_READ, CACHE_NONE | NO_EXCEPTIONS);
202: 		if (c=='\n' || c=='\t' || c=='\r' || (c>=' ' && c<127)) {
203: 			cur_length ++;
204: 			if (cur_length >= min_string_length)
205: 				return 1;
206: 		} else {
207: 			if (cur_length >= min_string_length)
208: 				return 1;
209: 			else
210: 				return 0;
211: 		}
212: 	}
213: }

306: /*
307:  *  cpu_create_or_reset_tc():
308:  *
309:  *  Create the translation cache in memory (ie allocate memory for it), if
310:  *  necessary, and then reset it to an initial state.
311:  */
312: void cpu_create_or_reset_tc(struct cpu *cpu)
313: {
314: 	size_t s = dyntrans_cache_size + DYNTRANS_CACHE_MARGIN;
315: 
316: 	if (cpu->translation_cache == NULL)
317: 		cpu->translation_cache = (unsigned char *) zeroed_alloc(s);
318: 
319: 	/*  Create an empty table at the beginning of the translation cache:  */
320: 	memset(cpu->translation_cache, 0, sizeof(uint32_t)
321: 	    * N_BASE_TABLE_ENTRIES);
322: 
323: 	cpu->translation_cache_cur_ofs =
324: 	    N_BASE_TABLE_ENTRIES * sizeof(uint32_t);
325: 
326: 	/*
327: 	 *  There might be other translation pointers that still point to
328: 	 *  within the translation_cache region. Let's invalidate those too:
329: 	 */
330: 	if (cpu->invalidate_code_translation != NULL)
331: 		cpu->invalidate_code_translation(cpu, 0, INVALIDATE_ALL);
332: }

regress/gate_crossfamily.sh
1: #!/bin/bash
2: # GATE 5 -- non-MIPS CPU cores actually execute guest code.
3: #
4: # float_emul.c is shared by the alpha, m88k, mips, ppc and sh cores, but until this gate
5: # existed only MIPS had ever executed it under test. Round 51 instrumented the old
6: # 20-machine smoke and measured that it performed ZERO floating-point stores.
7: #
8: # WHAT THIS GATE DOES AND DOES NOT PROVE. It proves the m88k and SH4 cores execute a full
9: # guest to a shell and return CHECKED ANSWERS -- "the guest kept running" is not a result,
10: # because a wrong float_emul.c arm keeps the guest running perfectly.
11: #
12: # It does NOT prove #287. The luna88k check computes 1.5/3.0 and sqrt(2), and gate 2's own
13: # closed form says old and new can only differ at |x| >= 2^128 or |x| < 2^-126. Both of
14: # those values are far inside the region where the two implementations agree, so reverting
15: # #287 would leave this gate green. That was claimed as #287 coverage in an earlier draft
16: # and it was wrong. #287 is covered by gate 2, which links the real float_emul.c; this gate
17: # covers the cores that CALL it. The S-format overflow arm has no in-guest coverage on any
18: # rig -- see images.md.
19: set -u
20: HERE=$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)
21: . "$HERE/lib.sh"
22: 
23: GX=${GX:-$ROOT/build/gxemul}
24: 
42:     #  a4: bracket the driver so the gate knows an interval that STRICTLY CONTAINS the
43:     #  driver's own.  A containment row holds under any host load -- both intervals dilate
44:     #  together -- which is how BOOT_WALL gets a real check without a wall-clock THRESHOLD.
45:     local t0w=$(date +%s)
46:     python3 -u "$HERE/drive_guest.py" "$rig" "$GX" > "$out" 2>&1
47:     local t1w=$(date +%s)
48:     sed 's/^/       /' "$out"
49: 
50:     v() { grep -E "^$1=" "$out" | head -1 | cut -d= -f2-; }
51:     # *** vall() EXISTS BECAUSE v() TAKES head -1. *** A rig declaring two expect_values had
52:     # only its FIRST compared literally: landisk prints VALUES=SH7751R and VALUES=42, and the
53:     # second rode on the aggregate VERDICT alone. The records claimed this was "not a live
54:     # false pass today -- landisk declares one value"; landisk declares TWO, so the record
55:     # contradicted itself and the false half was the exculpatory one. Comparing the joined
56:     # list makes every declared answer load-bearing.
57:     vall() { grep -E "^$1=" "$out" | cut -d= -f2- | paste -sd, -; }
58:     check "$rig: reached boot milestone" "$(v BOOT_REACHED)" "1"
59:     check "$rig: every computed answer"  "$(vall VALUES)"    "$want"
60:     check "$rig: verdict"                "$(v VERDICT)"      "PASS"
61: 
62:     # #420: the boot milestone is now waited for against a budget of GUEST WORK rather than
110:     # cannot see at all, so the driver prints them and these rows bound them.
111:     #
112:     # The bounds are derived, not taste. The stall threshold must be many -N record
113:     # intervals (worst measured gap on this path under saturation: 7.06 s) and must stay
114:     # well under the backstop, or a hang is indistinguishable from a slow host. The backstop
115:     # must exceed the worst boot measured -- 720.8 s here under 8 busy loops on 8 cores --
116:     # with real margin, and must not be so large that a wedged rig ties up the battery.
117:     local st bs bg
118:     st=$(v STALL); bs=$(v BACKSTOP); bg=$(v BUDGET)
119: 
120:     # *** ASSERT THE KEYS ARE PRESENT BEFORE INTERPRETING THEM. ***
121:     # Deleting the driver's `print("BUDGET=...")` produced ZERO red rows and then printed,
122:     # green, "budget is deliberately absent (uncalibrated rig)" for a rig whose budget is
123:     # 12 G -- because `${bg:-0}` turns MISSING into DELIBERATELY ZERO. That is worse than a
124:     # missed mutant: a guard row that states a falsehood while passing. STALL and BACKSTOP
125:     # default safely (they go red), but a default that happens to be safe is not a check.
126:     check "$rig: driver reported all three progress constants" \
127:           "$([ -n "$st" ] && [ -n "$bs" ] && [ -n "$bg" ] && echo yes || echo "missing: st='$st' bs='$bs' bg='$bg'")" yes
128:     #  a4: BOOT_WALL is TELEMETRY -- these two rows assert presence and containment, NEVER
doc\machine_decstation.html:356:GXemul on an Alpha 21164PC, with displays on a Sun Ultra1 (to the left),
doc\machine_decstation.html:357:on the Alpha itself (in the middle), and on an HP700/RX X-terminal (8-bit
doc\machine_decstation.html:358:color depth, running off the Alpha) to the right.

571: /*
572:  *  machine_add_devices_as_symbols():
573:  *
574:  *  Registers device addresses as symbols, to make the debugging experience
575:  *  somewhat nicer. offset should be e.g. 0xffffffff80000000 and
576:  *  0xffffffffa0000000 on MIPS.
577:  */

**VERDICT: Yes—this fork has a source-supported Alpha cold-debugger path on EB164 and AlphaServer 4100. All four described defects are cold-probe candidates. “Nothing Alpha can execute” is not a supported reason to close them.**

This is a static, read-only assessment; I did not build or execute a probe.

**Q1. Four Alpha subtypes are registered**, selected with `-E alpha` and the corresponding `-e` alias:

| Machine | Alias | Default CPU | Relevant implementation |
|---|---|---|---|
| AlphaBook 1 | `alphabook1` | 21066 | LCA/PCI/ISA devices; incomplete interrupt support |
| AlphaServer 4100 | `alphaserver4100` | 21164A-2 | Minimal setup; sufficient CPU/RAM infrastructure for cold execution |
| DEC 3000/300 | `3000/300` | 21064 | Constructor blocked by invalid interrupt wiring |
| EB164 | `eb164` | 21164PC | Minimal setup; sufficient CPU/RAM infrastructure for cold execution |

All default to 128 MB. These come from the actual [machine definitions and registration](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/machines/machine_alpha.c:203). `MACHINE_BAREALPHA` and `MACHINE_TESTALPHA` exist as constants, but there are **no registered `barealpha` or `testalpha` machines**.

There is **no mandatory external PROM image or hard-coded Alpha firmware filename** in these descriptions. The common Alpha setup synthesizes restart/console structures and PROM-call stubs in RAM. Its NetBSD bootstrap state is explicitly incomplete. I found **no established full-OS Alpha boot path in the allowed documentation or regression scripts**. That is narrower than proving every possible OS boot impossible. [Alpha bootstrap setup](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/machines/machine_alpha.c:83)

The DEC 3000/300 blocker is independently visible: its serial device receives literal `irq=0`; the device connects that named interrupt, while the Alpha CPU registers its qualified CPU path. The unresolved lookup aborts during construction, before the debugger. [Machine wiring](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/machines/machine_alpha.c:68), [serial initialization](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_z8530.c:212), [lookup failure](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/core/interrupt.c:181)

**Q2. The harness exercises Alpha construction, but supplies no Alpha guest-execution test.**

Across the 96 immediate `regress/*.py` and `regress/*.sh` files, Alpha does occur. The ASan sweep enumerates machine/subtype combinations from `-H`, launches them with a dummy ELF, and explicitly exempts `alpha/3000/300` from its construction-death check. The dummy loader failure is accepted; this does not establish instruction execution. [Exemption](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:175), [enumeration and launch](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/gate_asan_sweep.sh:219)

The cross-family gate’s introductory Alpha mention also does not mean Alpha execution coverage: its stated guest coverage is m88k and SH4. [Gate scope](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/gate_crossfamily.sh:2)

**Q3. Yes: the cold-debugger machinery connects to a real Alpha execution path.**

The relevant chain is present:

- Startup requires a loadable input, but the generic raw loader accepts ordinary bytes and supplies an entry point. An OS kernel or disk image is unnecessary. Existing ARM probes use this same bootstrap arrangement. [Raw loader](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/file/file_raw.c:37), [existing probe](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/arm_flags_probe.py:10)
- Alpha installs real memory, translation, and instruction-execution callbacks, and exposes PC and general registers to debugger settings. Initial kernel-mode translation supports RAM access before page tables exist. [CPU initialization](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/cpu_alpha.c:84), [bootstrap translation](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/memory_alpha.c:61)
- Debugger word writes use the memory helpers. Alpha fetches and decodes instructions through its actual memory path and includes the shared breakpoint machinery. Continuing resumes machine execution. [Debugger writes](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/debugger/debugger_cmds.c:1528), [Alpha fetch/decode](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/cpu_alpha_instr.c:775), [breakpoint handling](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/cpu_dyntrans.c:1795)

Thus EB164 or AlphaServer 4100 provides the required unmodified machine description. There is no architectural requirement to boot an OS first.

**Q4. Classification, matching your descriptions to the source sites:**

| Defect | Category | Relevant qualification |
|---|---|---|
| Page-table walk drops within-page offset | **Cold-debugger reachable** | Requires page-table state; ordinary bootstrap identity mapping bypasses the walk. The successful leaf translation returns only the frame base. [Source](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/memory_alpha.c:168) |
| PALcode register mismatch | **Cold-debugger reachable** | `swpctx` is an active handler reached by guest instruction dispatch. No OS dependency. [Source](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/cpu_alpha_palcode.c:229) |
| Unsupported fault-delivery claim | **Cold-debugger reachable** | The failed data-access path returns failure without delivering an Alpha exception. The load/store handler then abandons the access; its “already signalled a fault” comment is unsupported. [Memory failure](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/memory_rw.c:248), [instruction handling](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/cpu_alpha_instr_loadstore.c:68) |
| Guest-reachable process exit | **Cold-debugger reachable** | The bootstrap translator retains an explicit process exit on its special unsupported-address branch. [Source](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/memory_alpha.c:73) |

**Something else:** `swpctx` contains **two distinct inconsistencies**. It writes the old context to `a0` despite its “return … in v0” comment; separately, it assigns `new_ptbr` to PTBR and subsequently overwrites PTBR with `new_flags`. Your brief does not identify which mismatch was filed, so the other deserves explicit mention. Both are in active code. [Return register](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/cpu_alpha_palcode.c:250), [PTBR overwrite](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/cpu_alpha_palcode.c:275)
